# 32

## 32.1 TIM6/TIM7 introduction

The basic timers TIM6/TIM7 consist in a 16-bit autoreload counter driven by a programmable prescaler.

They can be used as generic timers for time-base generation.

The basic timer can also be used for triggering the digital-to-analog converter. This is done with the trigger output of the timer.

The timers are completely independent, and do not share any resources.

## 32.2 TIM6/TIM7 main features

Basic timer (TIM6/TIM7) features include:

16-bit autoreload upcounter.

16-bit programmable prescaler used to divide (also “on the fly”) the counter clock frequency by any factor between 1 and 65535.

Synchronization circuit to trigger the DAC.

Interrupt/DMA generation on the update event: counter overflow.

### 32.3.3 TIM6/TIM7 clocks

The timer bus interface is clocked by the tim\_pclk APB clock.

The counter clock tim\_ker\_ck is connected to the tim\_pclk input.

The CEN (in the TIMx\_CR1 register) and UG bits (in the TIMx\_EGR register) are actual control bits and can be changed only by software (except for UG that remains cleared automatically). As soon as the CEN bit is written to 1, the prescaler is clocked by the internal clock tim\_ker\_ck.

[Figure 508](#XREF_59122__Figure_476__Control_circuit_in_normal_mode%2C_internal_clock_div40) shows the behavior of the control circuit and the upcounter in normal mode, without prescaler.

<a id="XREF_59122__Figure_476__Control_circuit_in_normal_mode%2C_internal_clock_div40"></a>

Figure 508. Control circuit in normal mode, internal clock divided by 1![](MSv62317V2.svg)

### 32.3.4 Time-base unit

The main block of the programmable timer is a 16-bit upcounter with its related autoreload register. The counter clock can be divided by a prescaler.

The counter, the autoreload register and the prescaler register can be written or read by software. This is true even when the counter is running.

The time-base unit includes:

Counter register (TIMx\_CNT)

Prescaler register (TIMx\_PSC)

Auto-Reload register (TIMx\_ARR).

The autoreload register is preloaded. The preload register is accessed each time an attempt is made to write or read the autoreload register. The contents of the preload register are transferred into the shadow register permanently or at each update event UEV, depending on the autoreload preload enable bit (ARPE) in the TIMx\_CR1 register. The update event is sent when the counter reaches the overflow value and if the UDIS bit equals 0 in the TIMx\_CR1 register. It can also be generated by software. The generation of the update event is described in detail for each configuration.

The counter is clocked by the prescaler output tim\_cnt\_ck, which is enabled only when the counter enable bit (CEN) in the TIMx\_CR1 register is set.

Note that the actual counter enable signal tim\_cnt\_en is set one clock cycle after CEN bit set.

Prescaler description

The prescaler can divide the counter clock frequency by any factor between 1 and 65536. It is based on a 16-bit counter controlled through a 16-bit register (in the TIMx\_PSC register). It can be changed on the fly as the TIMx\_PSC control register is buffered. The new prescaler ratio is taken into account at the next update event.

[Figure 509](#XREF_29624__Figure_142__Counter_timing_diagram_with_prescaler_division_change_from_1_to_240) and [Figure 510](#XREF_13676__Figure_143__Counter_timing_diagram_with_prescaler_division_change_from_1_to_440) give some examples of the counter behavior when the prescaler ratio is changed on the fly.

<a id="XREF_29624__Figure_142__Counter_timing_diagram_with_prescaler_division_change_from_1_to_240"></a>

Figure 509. Counter timing diagram with prescaler division change from 1 to 2![](MSv50998V1.svg)

<a id="XREF_13676__Figure_143__Counter_timing_diagram_with_prescaler_division_change_from_1_to_440"></a>

Figure 510. Counter timing diagram with prescaler division change from 1 to 4![](MSv50999V1.svg)

### 32.3.5 Counting mode

The counter counts from 0 to the autoreload value (contents of the TIMx\_ARR register), then restarts from 0 and generates a counter overflow event.

An update event can be generate at each counter overflow or by setting the UG bit in the TIMx\_EGR register (by software or by using the slave mode controller).

The UEV event can be disabled by software by setting the UDIS bit in the TIMx\_CR1 register. This avoids updating the shadow registers while writing new values into the preload registers. In this way, no update event occurs until the UDIS bit has been written to 0, however, the counter and the prescaler counter both restart from 0 (but the prescale rate does not change). In addition, if the URS (update request selection) bit in the TIMx\_CR1 register is set, setting the UG bit generates an update event UEV, but the UIF flag is not set (so no interrupt or DMA request is sent).

When an update event occurs, all the registers are updated and the update flag (UIF bit in the TIMx\_SR register) is set (depending on the URS bit):

The buffer of the prescaler is reloaded with the preload value (contents of the TIMx\_PSC register).

The autoreload shadow register is updated with the preload value (TIMx\_ARR).

The following figures show some examples of the counter behavior for different clock frequencies when TIMx\_ARR = 0x36.

Figure 511. Counter timing diagram, internal clock divided by 1![](MSv50997V1.svg)

Figure 512. Counter timing diagram, internal clock divided by 2![](MSv62300V1.svg)

Figure 513. Counter timing diagram, internal clock divided by 4![](MSv62301V1.svg)

Figure 514. Counter timing diagram, internal clock divided by N![](MSv62302V1.svg)

Figure 515. Counter timing diagram, update event when ARPE = 0 (TIMx\_ARR not  
   preloaded)![](MSv62303V1.svg)

Figure 516. Counter timing diagram, update event when ARPE = 1 (TIMx\_ARR  
   preloaded)![](MSv62304V1.svg)

Dithering mode

The time base effective resolution can be increased by enabling the dithering mode, using the DITHEN bit in the TIMx\_CR1 register. This affects the way the TIMx\_ARR is behaving, and is useful for adjusting the average counter period when the timer is used as a trigger (typically for a DAC).

The operating principle is to have the actual ARR value slightly changed (adding or not one timer clock period) over 16 consecutive counting periods, with predefined patterns. This allows a 16-fold resolution increase, considering the average counting period.

[Figure 517](#XREF_76202__Figure_169__Dithering_principle40) presents the dithering principle applied to four consecutive counting periods.

<a id="XREF_76202__Figure_169__Dithering_principle40"></a>

Figure 517. Dithering principle![](MSv47466V1.svg)

When the dithering mode is enabled, the register coding is changed as follows (see [Figure 518](#XREF_41612__Figure_170__Data_format_and_register_coding_in_dithering_mode40) for example):

The four LSBs are coding for the enhanced resolution part (fractional part).

The MSBs are left-shifted to the bits 19:4 and are coding for the base value.

**Note:**

The ARR values are updated automatically if the DITHEN bit is set/reset (for instance, if ARR= 0x05 with DITHEN = 0, it is updated to ARR = 0x50 with DITHEN = 1). The following sequence must be followed when resetting the DITHEN bit:

1. CEN and ARPE bits must be reset

2. The ARR[3:0] bits must be reset

3. The DITHEN bit must be reset

4. The CEN bit can be set (eventually with ARPE = 1).

<a id="XREF_41612__Figure_170__Data_format_and_register_coding_in_dithering_mode40"></a>

Figure 518. Data format and register coding in dithering mode![](MSv45753V2.svg)

The minimum frequency is given by the following formula:

![RM0440_STM32G4xx_c7amba_gptimer2_BasicCfg_v4_UserSpec_image1137](RM0440_STM32G4xx_c7amba_gptimer2_BasicCfg_v4_UserSpec_image1137.png)

![RM0440_STM32G4xx_c7amba_gptimer2_BasicCfg_v4_UserSpec_image1139](RM0440_STM32G4xx_c7amba_gptimer2_BasicCfg_v4_UserSpec_image1139.png)

![RM0440_STM32G4xx_c7amba_gptimer2_BasicCfg_v4_UserSpec_image1141](RM0440_STM32G4xx_c7amba_gptimer2_BasicCfg_v4_UserSpec_image1141.png)

**Note:**

The maximum TIMx\_ARR value is limited to 0xFFFEF in dithering mode (corresponds to 65534 for the integer part and 15 for the dithered part).

As shown on [Figure 519](#XREF_81490__Figure_171__PWM_resolution_vs_frequency40), the dithering mode is used to increase the PWM resolution whatever the PWM frequency.

<a id="XREF_81490__Figure_171__PWM_resolution_vs_frequency40"></a>

Figure 519. FCnt resolution vs frequency![](MSv50910V1.svg)

The period changes are spread over 16 consecutive periods, as described in [Figure 520](#XREF_22045__Figure_171__PWM_dithering_pattern40).

<a id="XREF_22045__Figure_171__PWM_dithering_pattern40"></a>

Figure 520. PWM dithering pattern![](MSv47465V1.svg)

The autoreload and compare values increments are spread following the specific patterns described in [Table 321](#XREF_12462__Table_116__CCR_and_ARR_register_change_dithering_pattern40). The dithering sequence is done to have increments distributed as evenly as possible and minimize the overall ripple.

### 32.3.6 UIF bit remapping

The IUFREMAP bit in the TIMx\_CR1 register forces a continuous copy of the update interrupt flag UIF into the timer counter register’s bit 31 (TIMxCNT[31]). This is used to atomically read both the counter value and a potential roll-over condition signaled by the UIFCPY flag. In particular cases, it can ease the calculations by avoiding race conditions caused for instance by a processing shared between a background task (counter reading) and an interrupt (update interrupt).

There is no latency between the assertions of the UIF and UIFCPY flags.

### 32.3.7 ADC triggers

The timer can generate an ADC triggering event with various internal signals, such as reset, enable or compare events.

**Note:**

The clock of the slave peripherals (such as timer, ADC) receiving the tim\_trgo signal must be enabled prior to receiving events from the master timer, and the clock frequency (prescaler) must not be changed on-the-fly while triggers are received from the master timer.

### 32.3.8 TIM6/TIM7 DMA requests

The TIM6/TIM7 can generate a single DMA request, as shown in [Table 322](#XREF_61589__Table_643__DMA_request%11%5Bconditional_for_RM0440_only%5D40).


<div align="center" data-conditional="RM0440"><table class="TableTitle"><caption class="TableTitle"><p class="TableCap">Table 322. <a id="only%5D40" name="XREF_61589__Table_643__DMA_request%11%5Bconditional_for_RM0440_only%5D40">DMA request</a></p></caption><col width="165.20px"/><col width="104.37px"/><col width="123.86px"/><col width="144.32px"/><thead><tr height="28.00px"><th><p class="TableHeadC"><a id="5932503" name="353_TableHeadC_5932503"></a>DMA request signal</p></th><th><p class="TableHeadC">DMA acronym</p></th><th><p class="TableHeadC">DMA request</p></th><th><p class="TableHeadC">Enable control bit</p></th></tr></thead><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="TableBodyL">tim_upd_dma</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">TIM_UP</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyC">Update</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyC">UDE</p></td></tr></table></div>


### 32.3.9 Debug mode

When the microcontroller enters debug mode (Cortex®-M4 with FPU core halted), the TIMx counter can either continue to work normally or be stopped.

The behavior in debug mode can be programmed with a dedicated configuration bit per timer in the Debug support (DBG) module.

For more details, refer to section Debug support (DBG).

### 32.3.10 TIM6/TIM7 low-power modes

 


<div align="center"><table class="TableTitle"><caption class="TableTitle"><p class="TableCap">Table 323. <a id="RTC40" name="XREF_78061__Table_69__Effect_of_low_power_modes_on_RTC40">Effect of low-power modes on </a><span data-keyref="~gpt2BCv4_Timer#">TIM6/TIM7</span></p></caption><col width="67.45px"/><col width="471.13px"/><thead><tr height="28.00px"><th><p class="TableHeadC"><a id="1927674" name="353_TableHeadC_1927674"></a>Mode </p></th><th><p class="TableHeadC">Description </p></th></tr></thead><tr height="37.33px"><td><p class="TableBodyL">Sleep</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyC">No effect, peripheral is active. The interrupts can cause the device to exit from Sleep mode.</p></td></tr><tr height="37.33px"><td><p class="TableBodyL">Stop</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyC">The timer operation is stopped and the register content is kept. No interrupt can be generated.</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="TableBodyL">Standby</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyC">The timer is powered-down and must be reinitialized after exiting the Standby mode.</p></td></tr></table></div>


### 32.3.11 TIM6/TIM7 interrupts

The TIM6/TIM7 can generate a single interrupt, as shown in [Table 324](#XREF_80560__Table_294__Interrupt_request%1140).

## 32.4 TIM6/TIM7 registers

Refer to [Section 1.2](Documentation_conventions.md#XREF_82797__1_1_List_of_abbreviations_for_registers1) for a list of abbreviations used in register descriptions.

The peripheral registers can be accessed by half-words (16-bit) or words (32-bit).

Reset value: 0x0000

<div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="114.87px"/><col width="495.58px"/><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 15:13</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="73.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 12</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">DITHEN</span>: Dithering enable</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Dithering disabled</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Dithering enabled</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>The DITHEN bit can only be modified when CEN bit is reset.</p></td></tr><tr height="54.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 11</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">UIFREMAP</span>: UIF status bit remapping</p><p class="TableIndent">0: No remapping. UIF status bit is not copied to TIMx_CNT register bit 31.<br/>1: Remapping enabled. UIF status bit is copied to TIMx_CNT register bit 31.</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 10:8</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="54.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 7</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">ARPE</span>: Auto-reload preload enable</p><p class="TableIndent">0: TIMx_ARR register is not buffered.<br/>1: TIMx_ARR register is buffered.</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 6:4</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="54.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 3</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">OPM</span>: One-pulse mode</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Counter is not stopped at update event<br/>1: Counter stops counting at the next update event (clearing the CEN bit).</p></td></tr><tr height="153.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 2</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">URS</span>:<span style="font-weight:Bold;"> </span>Update request source</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set and cleared by software to select the UEV event sources.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Any of the following events generates an update interrupt or DMA request if enabled. These events can be: </p><p class="RegTBullet2">-<span style="padding-right:10pt ;">​</span>Counter overflow/underflow</p><p class="RegTBullet2">-<span style="padding-right:10pt ;">​</span>Setting the UG bit</p><p class="RegTBullet2">-<span style="padding-right:10pt ;">​</span>Update generation through the slave mode controller</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Only counter overflow/underflow generates an update interrupt or DMA request if enabled.</p></td></tr><tr height="169.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 1</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">UDIS</span>:<span style="font-style:Italic;"> </span>Update disable</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set and cleared by software to enable/disable UEV event generation.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: UEV enabled. The Update (UEV) event is generated by one of the following events:</p><p class="RegTBullet2">-<span style="padding-right:10pt ;">​</span>Counter overflow/underflow</p><p class="RegTBullet2">-<span style="padding-right:10pt ;">​</span>Setting the UG bit</p><p class="RegTBullet2">-<span style="padding-right:10pt ;">​</span>Update generation through the slave mode controller</p><p class="TableIndent">Buffered registers are then loaded with their preload values.</p><p class="TableIndent">1: UEV disabled. The Update event is not generated, shadow registers keep their value (ARR, PSC). However the counter and the prescaler are reinitialized if the UG bit is set or if a hardware reset is received from the slave mode controller.</p></td></tr><tr height="70.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">CEN</span>: Counter enable</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Counter disabled<br/>1: Counter enabled</p><p class="TableIndent">CEN is cleared automatically in one-pulse mode, when an update event occurs.</p></td></tr></table></div>


### 32.4.2 TIMx control register 2 (TIMx\_CR2)(x = 6 to 7)

Address offset: 0x04

Reset value: 0x0000

<div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="115.59px"/><col width="495.76px"/><tr data-conditional="_gpt2bcv4_4,_gpt2bcv4_5" height="22.67px" style="display: none"><td data-conditional="_gpt2bcv4_4,_gpt2bcv4_5" style="display: none"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 31:29</p></td><td data-conditional="_gpt2bcv4_4,_gpt2bcv4_5" style="display: none"><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_gpt2bcv4_4,_gpt2bcv4_5" height="101.33px" style="display: none"><td data-conditional="_gpt2bcv4_4,_gpt2bcv4_5" style="display: none"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 28</p></td><td data-conditional="_gpt2bcv4_4,_gpt2bcv4_5" style="display: none"><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">ADSYNC</span>: ADC synchronization</p><p class="TableIndent" style="margin-left : 17.01pt;">0: The timer operates independently from the ADC</p><p class="TableIndent" style="margin-left : 18.43pt;">1: The timer operation is synchronized with the ADC clock to provide jitter-free sampling point. This mode can be enabled only with specific ADC / timer clock relationship. Refer to <a href="#XREF_38242__66_3_8_ADC_synchronization" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;font-kerning:normal;font-stretch:normal;color:#0000ff;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Section 32.3.8</a> for requirements.</p><p class="TableIndent" style="margin-left : 18.43pt;">The ADSYNC must not modified when the counter is enabled (CEN bit is set).</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits <span data-conditional="_gpt2bcv4_0,_gpt2bcv4_1,_gpt2bcv4_2,_gpt2bcv4_3">15</span>:7</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="208.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 6:4</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">MMS[2:0]</span>: Master mode selection</p><p class="TableIndent">These bits are used to select the information to be sent in master mode to slave timers for synchronization (TRGO). The combination is as follows:</p><p class="TableIndent" style="margin-left : 28.35pt;">000:   <span style="font-weight:Bold;">Reset</span> - the UG bit from the TIMx_EGR register is used as a trigger output (tim_trgo).</p><p class="TableIndent" style="margin-left : 28.35pt;">001:   <span style="font-weight:Bold;">Enable</span> - the Counter enable signal, tim_cnt_en, is used as a trigger output (tim_trgo). It is useful to start several timers at the same time or to control a window in which a slave timer is enabled. The Counter Enable signal is generated when the CEN control bit is written.</p><p class="TableIndent" style="margin-left : 28.35pt;">010:   <span style="font-weight:Bold;">Update</span> - The update event is selected as a trigger output (tim_trgo). For instance a master timer can then be used as a prescaler for a slave timer.</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>The clock of the slave timer or he peripheral receiving the tim_trgo must be enabled prior to receive events from the master timer, and must not be changed on-the-fly while triggers are received from the master timer.</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 3:0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr></table></div>


### 32.4.3 TIMx DMA/Interrupt enable register (TIMx\_DIER)(x = 6 to 7)

Address offset: 0x0C

Reset value: 0x0000

<div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="113.77px"/><col width="497.49px"/><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 15:9</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="54.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 8</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">UDE</span>: Update DMA request enable</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Update DMA request disabled.<br/>1: Update DMA request enabled.</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 7:1</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="54.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">UIE</span>: Update interrupt enable</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Update interrupt disabled.<br/>1: Update interrupt enabled.</p></td></tr></table></div>

<div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="115.14px"/><col width="496.21px"/><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 15:1</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="120.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">UIF</span>: Update interrupt flag</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set by hardware on an update event. It is cleared by software.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: No update occurred.<br/>1: Update interrupt pending. This bit is set by hardware when the registers are updated:</p><p class="TableBullet" style="margin-left : 17.01pt;text-indent : -8.50pt;">–On counter overflow if UDIS = 0 in the TIMx_CR1 register.</p><p class="TableBullet" style="margin-left : 17.01pt;text-indent : -8.50pt;">–When CNT is reinitialized by software using the UG bit in the TIMx_EGR register, if URS = 0 and UDIS = 0 in the TIMx_CR1 register.</p></td></tr></table></div>

<div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="113.32px"/><col width="498.03px"/><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 15:1</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="85.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">UG</span>: Update generation</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit can be set by software, it is automatically cleared by hardware.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: No action.<br/>1: Re-initializes the timer counter and generates an update of the registers. Note that the prescaler counter is cleared too (but the prescaler ratio is not affected).</p></td></tr></table></div>


### 32.4.6 TIMx counter (TIMx\_CNT)(x = 6 to 7)

Address offset: 0x24

Reset value: 0x0000 0000

<div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="113.68px"/><col width="498.79px"/><tr height="54.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 31</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">UIFCPY</span>: UIF copy</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is a read-only copy of the UIF bit of the TIMx_ISR register. If the UIFREMAP bit in TIMx_CR1 is reset, bit 31 is reserved and read as 0.</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 30:16</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="102.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 15:0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">CNT[15:0]</span>: Counter value</p><p class="TableIndent" style="text-decoration: underline;">Non-dithering mode (DITHEN = 0)</p><p class="TableIndent">The register holds the counter value.</p><p class="TableIndent" style="text-decoration: underline;">Dithering mode (DITHEN = 1)</p><p class="TableIndent">The register only holds the non-dithered part in CNT[15:0]. The fractional part is not available.</p></td></tr></table></div>

<div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="113.55px"/><col width="497.37px"/><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 31:20</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="150.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 19:0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">ARR[19:0]</span>: Auto-reload value</p><p class="TableIndent">ARR is the value to be loaded into the actual auto-reload register.</p><p class="TableIndent">Refer to <a href="#XREF_89982__14_3_1_Time-base_unit40" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;font-kerning:normal;font-stretch:normal;color:#0000ff;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Section 32.3.4: Time-base unit</a> for more details about ARR update and behavior.</p><p class="TableIndent">The counter is blocked while the auto-reload value is null.</p><p class="TableIndent" style="text-decoration: underline;">Non-dithering mode (DITHEN = 0)</p><p class="TableIndent">The register holds the auto-reload value in ARR[15:0]. The ARR[19:16] bits are reserved.</p><p class="TableIndent" style="text-decoration: underline;">Dithering mode (DITHEN = 1)</p><p class="TableIndent">The register holds the integer part in ARR[19:4]. The ARR[3:0] bitfield contains the dithered part.</p></td></tr></table></div>
